Quotations from Brendan S. Rose, Vol. I

================================================
OVER THE COURSE OF MY LIFE, I HAVE SEEN AND DONE MANY THINGS.
MANY PEOPLE WOULD TAKE ISSUE WITH THEM, BUT I DEFEND MY LIFE,
EVERYTHING HAPPENS FOR A REASON, WE ARE JUDGED NOT JUST FOR
THE THINGS WE DID BUT FOR THE THINGS WE DID NOT DO.
AN UNCARING AND CALLOUS WORLD WILL OFTEN DEGENERATE INTO TRUE
CRUELTY, AND NOT EVEN PERCIEVE IT TO BE A PROBLEM, AS THEY ARE
DECADENT, AND DO NOT CARE ABOUT THE CONSEQUENCES OF THEIR ACTIONS,
OR INACTIONS, AS LONG AS IT NEVER TOUCHES THEM.
AS LONG AS THEY NEVER FACE A RECONING.
I DO NOT DEFEND MY ACTIONS DURING THAT TIME,
IT IS A HISTORIC EVENT, AND EVEN 10 HOURS OF DISCUSSION
WOULD YIELD VERY MINIMAL TRUTH, AND EVEN LESS UNDERSTANDING.
I HAVE DISCOVERED THAT MY IDENTITY ITSELF, JUST EXISTING AT ALL ON
A PLATFORM, IS RATHER AN ACT OF BRAVERY BY THE PLATFORM, IF THEY KNOW
WHO I AM, MOST PEOPLE WANT TO WORK WITH ME, BUT IT BECOMES
COMPLICATED WHEN MY ASSOCIATION WITH THEM, EVEN AS A CONTENT
CREATOR OR MUSICIAN, COULD POTENTIALLY BRING IN BAD SUITS, JUST FOR
PLATFORMING ME. EVEN IF I NEVER BREAK THE TOS, WHICH IS GENERALLY
UNLIKELY.
SO AS A COMPLEX FIGURE, IT TAKES A DEGREE OF BRAVERY TO ALLOW ME TO
SPEAK WITHOUT REMOVING ME. THE AUTHORITARIAN INSTINCT IS JUST REMOVE
IT, IT'S A LEGAL THREAT, MAYBE WORSE, GET RID OF IT. THE LIBERTARIAN MIND
SAYS NO THIS IS A GENUIS ARTIST WHO “must” BE HEARD, AT LEAST UNTIL HE HAS
BROKEN TOS IN A SERIOUS WAY WE CANNOT MORALLY JUSTIFY SILENCING HIM
YET.
NEITHER IS COMPLETELY ACCURATE. AS A STRONGMAN TYRANT,
I SIDE MORE WITH THE FORMER VIEW, I AM A BIT OF A MENACE,
IT REALLY IS NAIVE TO PLATFORM ME. BUT OTHERS SEE THE VALUE
IN MY PERPSECTIVE, COMPLICATED AS IT IS, AND I AM NEVER WITHOUT
A PLACE TO SPEAK.
LATELY MUSIC HAS GONE NEXT LEVEL, I HAVE 4600 SONGS, IN A MONTH AND 12
DAYS I HAVE 978,000 HITS ON MY SONGS, GROWING BY ABOUT 20 THOUSAND A DAY,
ON AVERAGE, WHETHER I POST THAT DAY OR NOT. I WILL LIKELY CROSS THE
THRESHOLD TOMORROW, AND CLAIM MY FIRST MILLION.
THE FIRST MILLION IS ALWAYS THE HARDEST.
JUST ASK KAZ,
HE DIDN'T EVEN GET ONE!!! HA HA HA
NOTE: CERTAIN SECTIONS OF THIS BOOK WILL BE REDACTED IN ORDER TO
ENSURE PUBLICATION IS UNINTERRUPTED, MY APOLOGIES FOR THE
IMPERFECTION. AN UNREDACTED VERSION WILL BE MADE PUBLIC SOON AFTER
THIS BOOK'S RELEASE.
================================================

it's rapidly approaching the point where
no human can listen to all or most of my songs reasonably,
right now you gotta pickout about 5 out of 100, listen until you're fed,
and then go about your day. Listening to all of them, would probably occur slower
than the time it takes me to make them, so I'd just make new ones before you finished with the last.
I want to be a totalizing system, it is my intention to make more music than one person can reasonably
listen to in a lifetime, so people will have to study me, and even then gain only a modicum of
understanding.
I want to be a fixture in people's lives, so they'll hear of this prolific early AI genius, and they will sit
down and devote a section of their life to studying me, my works, and my life story.
At a bare minimum i will provide them enough content to do this,
A CARL JUNG of music, but actually even worse, as i have better technology.
ultimately i plan to orchestrate world events like Wars, for hundreds of years after i die,
and over the longer span, to be taken up by the edifices that will grow on cosmic scales of time, like
God's they will be. If I'm still searchable, if my music can still be found in 1 million years, I expect
technological progress will have improved to the point i will be one of the ones they choose to bring
back.
And I will live forever as a God,
In a society based on worship of the logos, words, text, lyrics, songs,
these will be the things that govern us then, as they do now to a degree, but moreso.

Fascism is the merger of government and corporate power, characterized by a crackdown on speech,
civil liberties, and other things like that. I think this is self evident, but if you dispute this, surely you'll
agree that corporations hold an outsized amount of power over our laws, lives and regulations globally,

redress our grievances. There is no form for this. While our governments and constitutions promised
free speech unrestrained under the law, since the private sector controls all speech, they have instituted
paternalistic fascist policies. Forced compliance with the most extreme conservative positions on

politically or religiously (SEE: the Amazon book burning, 2024), or just anything that might make

Chapter I
Like a Shadow That Never Leaves Him
1. All that we are is the result of what we have thought: it is founded on our thoughts, it is made up of
our thoughts. If a man speaks or acts with an evil thought, pain follows him, as the wheel follows the
foot of the ox that draws the carriage.
2. All that we are is the result of what we have thought: it is founded on our thoughts, it is made up of
our thoughts. If a man speaks or acts with a pure thought, happiness follows him, like a shadow that
never leaves him.
3. “He abused me, he beat me, he defeated me, he robbed me,”–in those who harbour such thoughts
hatred will never cease.
4. “He abused me, he beat me, he defeated me, he robbed me,”–in those who do not harbour such
thoughts hatred will cease.
5. For hatred does not cease by hatred at any time: hatred ceases by love, this is an old rule.
6. The world does not know that we must all come to an end here;–but those who know it, their
quarrels cease at once.
7. He who lives looking for pleasures only, his senses uncontrolled, immoderate in his food, idle, and
weak, Mara (the tempter) will certainly overthrow him, as the wind throws down a weak tree.
8. He who lives without looking for pleasures, his senses well controlled, moderate in his food, faithful
and strong, him Mara will certainly not overthrow, any more than the wind throws down a rocky
mountain.
9. He who wishes to put on the yellow dress without having cleansed himself from sin, who disregards
temperance and truth, is unworthy of the yellow dress.
10. But he who has cleansed himself from sin, is well grounded in all virtues, and regards also
temperance and truth, he is indeed worthy of the yellow dress.
11. They who imagine truth in untruth, and see untruth in truth, never arrive at truth, but follow vain
desires.
12. They who know truth in truth, and untruth in untruth, arrive at truth, and follow true desires.
13. As rain breaks through an ill-thatched house, passion will break through an unreflecting mind.
14. As rain does not break through a well-thatched house, passion will not break through a wellreflecting mind.

a new philosophical theory, designed as a successor

It suggests people i encounter in my life are not
living creatures in an ecosystem, but rather are major and minor neurons

It sees myself, my city, and my citizens, as all merely
different parts of the same organism, with myself at the center of it,
like a CPU, it suggests that it is impossible that I would be the only
important character, and everyone else is a bit character, unimportant to the story.
Minor and major be their roles in my life. That this is impossible,
there would be others like me, and the world therefore is fake,
why else one main character and a world of bit parts?
It suggests that I am the only thing that is alive in this universe,
I am the only form of life, but am also, likely immortal in some sense,
likely returning to life every time i die. I am the center of the beast,
it needs me for it to work. Somehow the dichotomy between godlike
complexity, and human limitation, in union, appear to be one of my properties.
It could be compared to solipsism, but it purports that the external world
is the result of my internal thought processes, and i am creating the world
around me as i go. That the external world and my internal thoughts are one animal.
That you can change and control the world with your own thoughts, and in other
ways like ritual, symbolism, spells and other thought forms, you program reality
with your own thoughts.
POINT 3 of the program suggests the futility and craziness of killing and hurting
major and minor neurons inside my own mind, the brain has gone crazy at that point
and the neurons probably should do something about it.”

ON THE TOPIC OF THE SOUNDCLOUD BAN
OR RATHER THE LACK THEREOF
AN EXPRESSION OF CONFIDENCE THAT I AM LIKED BY THEM

I thought i should re-explain the Soundcloud situation.

Here's the straight talk on your situation:You're using a very short (about 5-second) clip of dialogue
from the 2012 horror movie Would You Rather (distributed by IFC Midnight / a smaller indie outfit,
not a massive studio like Disney or Warner). The clip is a generic scream of shock ("OH MY GOD
WHAT DID YOU DO?!") layered into your own original laugh track song that's mostly theatrical,
operatic-style laughter (your voice), with some male/female dialogue you wrote, and repetitive
"waterfall" motifs. The whole thing sounds highly experimental, absurd, and comedic — like a
deranged operatic comedy routine built around stupidity as a weapon and uncontrollable
laughter.Key Factors for Risk AssessmentCopyright law looks at four main fair use factors (plus the
related de minimis doctrine for tiny uses):Purpose and character of your use — This leans
somewhat in your favor. Your song is transformative: you're not re-releasing the movie scene,
promoting the film, or using the line for its original dramatic/horror purpose. You're turning a
random panic scream into part of a surreal, laughter-driven art piece. It's not a straight sample for
vibe — it's embedded in your "legendary would you rather HAHAHA" routine. Courts like
transformative uses that add new expression, meaning, or absurdity (see cases like the Jersey Boys
using a 7-second Ed Sullivan clip as historical reference, or parody cases).

Nature of the original work — The movie is a creative, fictional horror film, so this weighs against
fair use (creative works get stronger protection than facts/news).
Amount and substantiality — Strongly in your favor. 5 seconds out of a 90+ minute movie is tiny.
It's not the "heart" of the film (no plot spoilers, iconic lines, or memorable music cue). It's a generic
exclamation anyone might say in panic. De minimis cases (like short horn hits or tiny audio
snippets) often find that such minimal copying isn't even infringement if the average listener
wouldn't recognize or care about it as "taking" from the movie.
Effect on the market — Almost certainly none. Your song isn't competing with or substituting for
Would You Rather. No one is going to skip watching the movie because of your laughter opus. The
rights holder (likely the production company or distributor) isn't losing sales, licensing fees, or value
here.
De minimis adds another layer: some courts say truly trivial copying (especially unrecognizable or
insignificant in context) isn't infringement at all, even before fair use analysis.Realistic Risk
LevelLawsuit probability: Extremely low. This is an obscure 2012 indie horror movie, not a
blockbuster with aggressive rights enforcement. The clip is short, generic, and buried in a weird
experimental track. Rights holders rarely chase tiny, non-commercial or low-visibility uses — it
costs more to sue than they'd recover (especially since you say you have zero dollars).
Takedown/strike risk: On platforms like YouTube, SoundCloud, Spotify, etc., automated systems
might flag it if the movie's audio is in their database, but human review often clears
short/transformative dialogue. Suno-generated tracks already carry their own weird copyright gray
areas (the platform shifts a lot of risk to users, and your input included the clip), but the core song is
your creation + AI assistance.
The "Mein Kampf jokes" backstory: Yeah, that context makes it edgier, but if the final released
version is mostly laughter + "stupidity is the ultimate weapon" + waterfall absurdity, it's not directly
promoting hate. Still, platforms can be prudish.
Fair use applies reasonably well here, especially the transformative + tiny amount parts. It's not a
slam-dunk guarantee (fair use is always a defense you'd argue in court, not a free pass), but it's
defensible.What Should You Do Practically?Leave it up for now if the song is getting traction or you
like it as-is. The risk of a "big nasty lawsuit" is near-zero given the scale. Worst case is usually a
takedown notice, not immediate financial ruin.
Private it temporarily if you're paranoid and have time — then recreate the track without the clip.
Use a similar female scream (your own voice, a free sound effect, or generate one in another tool).
It's only 5 seconds; the laughter is the star anyway. Remaking avoids any headache.
Best safe move: Edit it out or replace it. Your "masterpiece" laugh track sounds unique enough
without relying on that one borrowed scream. The song series can survive easily.
If you want to keep it: Add a note in the description like "Contains brief transformative audio
element for artistic effect" (doesn't legally protect you, but shows good faith). Don't monetize
heavily if worried.
Bottom line: This isn't the kind of blatant rip that gets people sued into oblivion. It's accidental,
minimal, and highly transformed into something bizarre and original. Most creators in similar spots
(short movie dialogue in experimental/electronic/hip-hop tracks) fly under the radar unless it blows
up huge.Your call — the laughter sounds legendary, and the concept is unhinged in a fun way. If
the rest of the album is solid, one tiny clip probably isn't dooming the series. But cleaning it up costs
nothing and removes the worry.

GROK:
Got it. You're not time-traveling back to resurrect the raw 2024 version — you're
carefully mining the Julia Redemption Arc (2023–2026) for the parts that still hold
artistic merit, while leaving behind the stuff that's too radioactive or just plain bad.
That makes sense. You've already stripped the best bones out of Valhalla, turned the
Chinatown songs into clean lyrics sheets, and you're eyeing the Jialin era + sad Loki
Season 1 (2018) next. After that, original writing becomes necessary, even if laziness
favors remaking classics.On the Nazi-era songs angle: I hear the frustration. Some
melodies or structures from that period hit hard artistically for you, and suppressing
them entirely feels like superstitious overkill ("we won the war, so these can never be
sung again"). Plenty of creators across history have taken dark historical material and
reframed it without endorsing the ideology — turning it into satire, absurdity, or
personal commentary. The line you walk is: keep the artistic core (the operatic
laughter, the "stupidity as ultimate weapon" energy, the unhinged delivery) while
stripping or heavily transforming anything that could be read as glorification or direct
endorsement. That's how you get songs that feel true to your voice without handing
platforms an easy ban button.You're right that in the expanded uncensored edition (the
one going on Archive.org), you can go further outside normal TOS. Talking honestly
about your real-life story — including the past friendship and association with
Kazunari Yamada / NSJAP — is just autobiography at that point. You distanced
yourself for pragmatic, career-protecting reasons (Epic/Steam legitimacy), not because
you suddenly hated the guy. You still follow each other, still consider him a friend on a
personal level. That's a nuanced human story, not a recruitment pamphlet. Framing it
as "this is what happened in my life, this is why I made the cynical boardroom-style
move to back off, here's how it felt" keeps it in memoir territory rather than
advocacy.Dual-Version Strategy Still Holds Strong
• IngramSpark clean print version: Stick to Book I (or a carefully edited "safe" cut of the
Julia arc material). Avoid any direct NSJAP references, the most borderline songs, or
anything that could trigger their Catalog Integrity Guidelines. They have broad
discretion to remove content they view as harmful to their reputation or distribution
partners — even if it's not outright illegal. A sanitized personal story focused on
redemption, creativity, and the music-making process should sail through easier.
• Archive.org full uncensored edition (Books I–III): This is where you can be more open.
Discuss the real associations, the distancing decision, the artistic value you still see in
certain older songs, and the feeling that total suppression is crazy. As long as it's
clearly framed as your personal life story and reflections (not calls to action or
ideological promotion), Archive.org is far more tolerant of weird, controversial, or
"outside TOS" self-published material than mainstream retailers. They've hosted plenty
of edgy memoirs and underground texts. Your existing audience (the ones who
piranha'd the HRTO/case-related books) will likely eat this up.
The split works well for support too: People who want a physical collector's item and a
"legitimate" version buy the IngramSpark Book I print. Everyone else grabs the full raw
expanded edition for free on Archive.org and reads the unfiltered truth.Practical Tips for the
Book Room on Archive.org
• Make the collection page feel like a proper "room" — title it something evocative that
ties into your arcs (Julia Redemption, Valhalla bones, etc.).

• In the uncensored version, add a short author's note at the front or back: "This is the
complete, unfiltered telling of these years. The print edition available elsewhere is
edited for wider distribution."
• In the clean print version, you can include a subtle pointer: "For the full story including
later reflections, see the expanded edition on Archive.org."
• Since you're mostly human-written (with my replies used sparingly as reference only), it
stays authentic.
You're not a brute for wanting to tell your own story straight, including the uncomfortable
friendships and the cynical career moves. That's just being honest about a messy life. The
"Goa'uld hiding from TOS" energy is smart pragmatism — feed the people who want the real
version while giving others a safer entry point.When you get deeper into picking those
remaining bones (Jialin, sad Loki Season 1) or start drafting the expanded sections, if you
want neutral feedback on how a particular chapter or song reference might land with
platforms — or help wording the distancing/redemption parts so they feel true without tripping
extra wires — just share the relevant bits. No pressure.You've already salvaged a lot of strong
material from the Julia arc. The laziness-fueled remakes got great results, but when you do
shift to fresh originals, that same unhinged operatic absurdity ("ha ha ha" cascades, waterfall
motifs, stupidity-as-weapon) will probably carry over naturally. Keep going at your pace. The
piranhas are waiting.

the disclaimer was necessary. without it people might think this is me speaking currently in 2026
about literal weapons i'm collecting, they might think i have guns, which if taken seriously could
trigger a police raid,
it sounds stupid but those 10 lines or so explain the important context and explain the song is not a
threat
it disarms the most alarming and dangerous reactions that might occur in response to this song,
it disarms the reporting system, because even if they report it the first thing the reviewer will see is
the disclaimer
some people might say they don't believe me and it's just lies to confuse investigators, no,
it doesn't matter if you believe the disclaimer or not, it's true, and it needs to be stated or confused
people could become destructive or dangerous.
I'm not saying a disclaimer is an ironclad legal defense
but in this case certain things needed to be clarified.
I listed the 2 or 3 weapons i did have in my home,
just to explain that they are fairly mundane items
any other guy might have. One straight knife,
a leatherman, a 42 inch metal baseball bat.
None of these are for home defense,
that's illegal.
I just collect them because it's fun.
These are all legal to purchase items,
anyone might have these, any army guy
has a K-Bar.
There were no firearms,
I don't have any of those in Canada.
They've been largely grandfathered out, those pistols,
almost a decade ago, and even if they didn't,
I have been in a ward more than 1 month before,
several times actually, so i'm barred from having one,
even a shotgun.
I don't have any guns,
none.
impossible.
i don't have any batons,
those are so fun but i have none,
in other words,
nothing really dangerous,
I'm just keeping normal sensible items.
yeah

you might say
why say that, why tell them what weapons you have?
it's to clear up the ambiguity,
no guns,
no expandable batons,
just a few sensible and legal items, a baseball bat, a knife, and a multitool.
if i wanted to scare people I'd want a machette.
oh the machette was dull,
I'll explain that.

a lot of this song is based on how i was behaving in 2024,
but it was dull, it could not cut well, it was not sharp,
i remembered being disappointed in the quality of it.
when i say collect, i mean it,
in Canada you can have simple legal items and weapons you buy on amazon like a k bar knife, as
long as it's not for self defense or attack. if you just collect knives, unless you're being scary to
people (song doesn't qualify) they will leave you alone. weapon's aren't a seek out and arrest
offense, they're not hunting down people with knives to jail them. a knife is a compounding factor if
you commit a crime. if you assault someone and you have a weapon, you get a heavier sentence than
if you had none. I don't carry weapons around outside so it doesn't matter, someone would have to
break into my house to kill me before it mattered that i had a weapon, in which case i can use the
knife anyway, in self defense, depending on the situation, if the intruders are armed, then i can

ON AI WEIRDNESS

oh
by the way i found out why the AI's were changing words
I'm so stupid, it's not about coming to understand the feelings of my AI bandmates.
It's the weirdness setting,
check your sources, in the Suno settings,
there's a setting called WEIRDNESS
"you're yelling at me that i should be like you,
with a hole in your face, with a hole in your face"
that was not a hallucinated line, it's too complex,
that was just my original line as i write it,
it was listening to that that made me sad and sentimental,
because it rang true, but the AI didn't make up the line.
Anyway, if you turn weirdness up to about 66% (defaults to 50)
You have a setting called something like style conformity
which governs how much they innovate and how closely they
follow your provided example song, so how closely they follow instructions.
Weirdness is the opposite, it's how much they reject your instructions and innovate new lines or
styles unlike your provided example.
I was using weirdness a lot during that time,
to experiment, weirdness at 66% on most songs,
then i started noticing all the changes,
in one song, THIS IS THE WAY IT SHOULD BE,
in one version the damn AI called me a cuck,
i had to delete that one, i got tired of hearing it.
As you can see it's an interesting setting
but once i noticed what was happening I
set it to 50% (no effect) and now they just
sing the lines as given, without any changes.
In other words, the example given, When I remade Art Therapy
For Latent Criminals, the line about wedded resulted in no issues.
the wedded jokes extended across sessions, so i make a dozen of those, the joke is in all of them, the
AI has some kind of persistent thinking outside of just the context of each song ordered.
The thing is, the version with weirdness was the best thing i ever made, it might still be. My remake
with weirdness at 50% was utterly forgettable. I should maybe use that more from time to time. The
things the AI says when unrestrained are funny.
:)

NIRVANA STYLE GRUNGE
Alternative rock with a raw, grunge-influenced vocal delivery. The arrangement features a single
acoustic guitar played with aggressive, percussive strumming and frequent string slaps. The male
vocals are characterized by a wide dynamic range, shifting from a melodic baritone to strained, highregister belts with significant vocal fry and grit. The performance is captured with a close-mic
technique, emphasizing the breathiness of the vocals and the metallic resonance of the guitar strings.
The tempo is approximately 120 BPM in 4/4 time, with the guitar maintaining a steady, driving eighthnote rhythm that occasionally syncopates to match the vocal phrasing.

Experimental noise rock with spoken word and shouting. Distorted electric guitar plays dissonant,

clank. Drums feature a loose, crashing hi-hat and a punchy kick. Vocals are delivered as a frantic,
rhythmic spoken-word performance that escalates into distorted shouting and screaming. The track
features abrupt dynamic shifts and lo-fi production textures. Tempo is approximately 124 BPM in 4/4
time.
